Full answer
-----------

Answer: $1,750/month ($1,500 for EDGX Top + $250 for BYX Top)

How the bill is calculated

| Feed | Host’s own users | Hosted subscribers (all non‑prof.) | Program status for host | Fees the host pays | |------|------------------|-----------------------------------|------------------------|-------------------| | EDGX Top | 5,000 (≤10,000, 100 % non‑prof.) | • Sub A: 4,000 EDGX only (≤10,000) <br>• Sub B: 4,000 EDGX (≤10,000) | In the Hosted Program – host has ≥1 eligible hosted subscriber, so its Distribution Fee is waived (credit of $750). | • Host Distribution Fee: $0 (waived) <br>• Sub A flat fee (in lieu of per‑user fees): $750 <br>• Sub B flat fee (EDGX portion): $750 <br>• EDGX Top total = $1,500 | | BYX Top | 5,000 (≤10,000, 100 % non‑prof.) | • Sub A: does not take BYX <br>• Sub B: 10,500 BYX (>10,000 cap) | Not in the Hosted Program for BYX – Sub B exceeds the 10,000‑user cap, so it must license BYX directly. The host has no remaining eligible hosted subscriber for BYX, so the Distribution Fee credit does not apply. | • Host Distribution Fee (Small Retail Broker rate): $250 <br>• Sub B is direct‑licensed; host pays nothing for it <br>• BYX Top total = $250 |

Key rules applied (from the filings)

- The cap for the Small Retail Broker / Hosted Programs was raised to 10,000 Non‑Professional Users per feed, measured per firm (host and each subscriber counted separately).
- A Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor that supplies data to at least one eligible External Hosted Subscriber receives a full credit of its Distribution Fee ($750 for EDGX Top; $250 for BYX Top).
- Each eligible External Hosted Subscriber receives:

– a waiver of its Distribution Fee, and – a flat monthly fee in lieu of Non‑Professional User fees ($750 for EDGX Top; $100 for BYX Top).

- Once a subscriber exceeds 10,000 users on a given feed, it loses program eligibility for that feed and must license directly with the exchange.

Because Sub B’s 10,500 BYX Top users break the BYX cap, Sub B is out of the BYX hosted program and licenses BYX directly. The host therefore retains only its own 5,000 BYX users, remains in the ordinary Small Retail Broker Distribution Program (paying the $250 discounted distribution fee), but does not earn the hosted‑program distribution‑fee waiver for BYX.

Total monthly bill for the two feeds: $1,500 (EDGX Top) + $250 (BYX Top) = $1,750

(This covers the program‑specific distribution and flat subscriber charges. The host’s own 5,000 non‑professional users on each feed are subject to the exchange’s standard per‑user fees, but those are unchanged by the program and are not part of the fee adjustments calculated here.)

Reasoning
---------

We need parse documents to determine fees for hosting broker with own users and hosted subscribers on EDGX Top and BYX Top, under Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program and updated eligibility requirements.

We need understand fee schedule for EDGX Top and BYX Top, including Small Retail Broker Distribution Program and new Hosted Solutions Program.

We have Document 5 (EDGX) and Document 6 (BYX). Need extract relevant fee provisions.

Let's analyze documents carefully.

Document 5: Cboe EDGX Exchange, Inc.; Notice of Filing and Immediate Effectiveness... proposed rule change to introduce Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program and update existing eligibility requirements for Small Retail Brokerage Distribution Program for Cboe One Summary Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed.

Document 6: Same but for BYX Top Data Feed and Cboe One Summary Feed.

We need determine monthly bill for host (hosting broker) for the two feeds (EDGX Top and BYX Top). Need consider host's own users and hosted subscribers.

We need understand program rules and fee amounts.

Let's extract key parts from Document 5 and 6.

Document 5 details:

- Applicable Feeds: EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data.
- Existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program provides discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed as well as discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data for eligible participants.
- Eligibility for existing program: External Distributor that meets: (i) broker-dealer distributing Applicable Feed to Non-Professional Data Users with brokerage relationship; (ii) At least 90% of Distributor's total subscriber population must consist of Non-Professional subscribers, inclusive of any subscribers not receiving Applicable Feed; (iii) Distributor distributes Applicable Feed to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users (proposing to increase to 10,000 for Cboe One Summary Data Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed).

Now proposed Program (Hosted Solutions Program):

Will provide fee waivers and lower data costs for both (i) Small Retail Brokers that provide Applicable Feeds to other Small Retail Brokers via hosted solutions (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) and (ii) the Small Retail Brokers that receive this data from Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor.

Specifically based on customer feedback, there are Small Retail Brokers who want to provide data via hosted solution as White Label Service ("Hosting Small Retail Broker Dealer") to other Small Retail Brokers, who then provide to retail clients ("External Hosted Subscriber").

Under existing structure, both External Hosted Subscriber and Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor are assessed standard discounted Distribution Fee (and for Cboe One Summary, discounted Data Consolidation Fee) under existing Small Retail Broker Program. These fees in addition to standard Professional and Non-Professional User fees. So existing fee structure does not allow additional benefits for Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors for providing service, and doesn't account for fact that Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors are also billed for fees of External Hosted Subscribers.

Of further note, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor responsible for reporting External Hosted Subscribers and users, ultimately responsible for payment of all data fees for both External Hosted Subscriber and itself.

Proposed pricing allows Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors freedom to charge or not charge External Hosted Subscribers.

Proposed Program details (need exact fee structures):

Under proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under existing Small Retail Broker Program.

Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for Cboe One Summary Data (credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data. The Professional User fees shall remain same.

Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for program and will be required to directly license with Exchange for Applicable Feed.

The Exchange notes 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on firm level (counts for each of Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers looked at separately). Additionally, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one exceeds threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue).

Also propose to modify existing Small Retail Broker Program for Cboe One Summary Feed and EDGX Top Feed to increase number of Non-Professional Data User maximum from 5,000 to 10,000 to be consistent with proposed threshold for External Hosted Subscribers.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: $875 per month ($500 for EDGX Top + $375 for BYX Top)

How the bill is calculated

| Item | EDGX Top | BYX Top | |------|----------|---------| | Host’s own non‑professional users | 5,000 | 5,000 | | Non‑professional user rate | $0.10/user/mo | $0.025/user/mo | | User fees for host’s own subscribers | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 | | Distribution fee (Small Retail Broker Program) | $750/mo | $250/mo | | Hosted‑solution credit? | Yes – host has 2 eligible hosted subscribers (A: 4,000 users; B: 4,000 EDGX users), both ≤10,000. Full credit of $750 applies. | No – the only BYX hosted subscriber (B) has 10,500 BYX users, exceeding the 10,000‑user cap. B is ineligible for the program and must license directly. With no other eligible BYX hosted subscriber, the host does not receive the $250 credit. | | Distribution fee paid by host | $750 – $750 = $0 | $250 | | Monthly total for feed | $500 | $375 |

Why the hosted subscribers are treated this way

- The eligibility cap is 10,000 non‑professional users per feed, counted separately for each firm (host vs. each hosted subscriber) [Doc 5 & 6].
- Subscriber A (EDGX only, 4,000 users) is within the cap → eligible for the hosted program; pays the flat $750/mo for EDGX Top (in lieu of per‑user fees).
- Subscriber B is within the cap for EDGX (4,000 users) but exceeds it for BYX (10,500 users). So B remains in the program for EDGX (flat $750/mo) but must directly license BYX and is not counted as an eligible hosted subscriber for BYX.
- Because B is ineligible for BYX, the host has zero eligible BYX hosted subscribers and therefore forfeits the BYX distribution‑fee credit. The host still qualifies for the base Small Retail Broker Program on its own 5,000 BYX users (≤10,000), so it pays the discounted $250 distribution fee.

No consolidation fees apply because only the Top feeds (EDGX Top and BYX Top) are involved—no Cboe One Summary feed is used.

Host’s total monthly bill: $500 (EDGX Top) + $375 (BYX Top) = $875.

Full answer

Answer: $875/month

How the fees work

| Feed | Normal non‑prof. user fee | Program flat fee (hosted subscriber) | Distribution fee (existing Small‑Retail Program) | Distribution‑fee credit (Hosted‑Solutions Program) | |------|---------------------------|--------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------| | EDGX Top | $0.10/user | $750/mo (replaces per‑user fees) | $750/mo | $750/mo (waived if host has ≥1 eligible hosted subscriber) | | BYX Top | $0.025/user | $100/mo (replaces per‑user fees) | $250/mo | $250/mo (waived if host has ≥1 eligible hosted subscriber) |

Eligibility rules (per‑firm, 10,000‑user cap)

- A hosting broker gets its distribution fee credited/waived for a feed only if it has at least one eligible hosted subscriber for that feed (≤10,000 users).
- If a hosted subscriber exceeds 10,000 users, it falls out of the program and must license directly; it no longer counts as an eligible hosted subscriber for that feed.
- The host’s own users are billed at the standard non‑professional per‑user rate (the flat fee applies only to the hosted subscriber).

Apply to the scenario

Host’s own users: 5,000 EDGX Top + 5,000 BYX Top (all non‑professional).

Hosted subscribers:

1. Only EDGX Top – 4,000 users (≤10,000) → eligible.
2. EDGX Top (4,000) + BYX Top (10,500) – EDGX part is eligible; BYX part is 10,500 (>10,000) → ineligible for BYX.

EDGX Top bill for the host

- Distribution fee: $0 (waived – host has two eligible EDGX hosted subscribers).
- Own users: 5,000 × $0.10 = $500.
- EDGX total = $500**

BYX Top bill for the host

- Hosted subscriber with BYX is over the cap and ineligible, so the host has no eligible BYX hosted subscriber.
- Distribution fee: $250 (not waived).
- Own users: 5,000 × $0.025 = $125.
- BYX total = $375**

Total monthly bill for both feeds

\[ \$500 \;+\; \$375 \;=\; \boxed{\$875} \]

Professional User fees remain same.

- The 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on firm level (counts for each Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers looked at separately).
- Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one exceeds 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program).

Full answer
-----------

Monthly bill breakdown

| Feed | Host’s own users | User-fee cost | Distribution fee | Hosted-subscriber effect | Feed total | |------|------------------|---------------|------------------|--------------------------|------------| | EDGX Top | 5,000 non-prof | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | $750 (retail discount) → $0 (waived because host has eligible hosted subscribers) | Both hosted subscribers are ≤10,000 users on EDGX, so host keeps the hosted-program credit | $500 | | BYX Top | 5,000 non-prof | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 | $250 (retail discount) → $250 (no hosted-program credit because the only BYX hosted subscriber has 10,500 users, exceeding the 10,000-user cap and is ineligible; that subscriber must license BYX Top directly) | Subscriber B is out of the program for BYX; Subscriber A takes no BYX | $375 |

Why this applies

- Existing Small Retail Broker Program (Document 5 for EDGX, Document 6 for BYX): gives a discounted distribution fee ($750 EDGX / $250 BYX) and requires ≤10,000 non-professional users. The host’s 5,000 own users meet that.
- New Hosted Solutions Program: gives the host a full credit of its distribution fee if it has at least one eligible hosted subscriber for that feed.
- EDGX: Subscriber A (4,000 users) and Subscriber B (4,000 EDGX users) are both ≤10,000, so the host’s $750 EDGX distribution fee is waived.
- BYX: Only Subscriber B takes BYX, but with 10,500 users he exceeds the 10,000-user cap, so he is ineligible for the BYX hosted program and must license directly. Thus the host has no eligible BYX hosted subscriber and does not get the $250 BYX distribution-fee credit (though it still pays the base retail-discounted $250 fee).
- User fees: The host pays standard non-professional per-user fees for its own subscribers ($0.10/EDGX; $0.025/BYX). The hosted subscribers pay their own flat fees ($750 EDGX; $100 BYX) or direct-license fees, which are not on the host’s bill.

Total host bill for both feeds

$500 (EDGX) + $375 (BYX) = $875 per month.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: $2,125 per month

How the bill breaks down

| Component | EDGX Top | BYX Top | |---|---|---| | Host’s own users (5,000 non‑prof. each) | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 | | Host distribution fee | $750 → waived (hosting credit) | $250 → waived (hosting credit) | | Hosted subscriber A (EDGX only, 4,000 users) | Flat program fee = $750 (dist. fee waived) | Does not take BYX → $0 | | Hosted subscriber B (4,000 EDGX; 10,500 BYX) | EDGX portion (4,000 ≤ 10,000) → flat $750 (dist. fee waived) | BYX portion (10,500 > 10,000) → exceeds cap; must license directly with the exchange, so not on host’s bill | | Subtotal per feed | $2,000 | $125 |

Total = $2,000 + $125 = $2,125

Key rules applied

- Under the Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program, a hosting broker that supplies data to at least one hosted subscriber receives a full credit of its own discounted distribution fee ($750 for EDGX Top; $250 for BYX Top).
- Each hosted subscriber within the 10,000‑user cap pays a flat monthly fee instead of per‑user charges: $750 for EDGX Top and $100 for BYX Top, and its own distribution fee is also waived.
- The 10,000‑user limit is applied per firm per feed. Subscriber B is over the cap on BYX (10,500 users) but under it on EDGX (4,000 users), so BYX for that subscriber falls out of the program and is billed directly to the subscriber, not through the host.
- All other users are non‑professional, so the standard per‑user rates apply only to the host’s own subscribers ($0.10 for EDGX; $0.025 for BYX). No Cboe One Summary consolidation fees apply here.

Reasoning
---------

We need parse problem: "A hosting broker has 5,000 users of its own on each of EDGX Top and BYX Top, and two hosted subscribers: one takes only EDGX Top, with 4,000 users; the other has 4,000 EDGX Top and 10,500 BYX Top users. All users are non-professional. What is the host's monthly bill for the two feeds?"

We need understand existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program and new Hosted Solutions Program.

Let's extract relevant fee provisions from document 5.

In document 5, Section "Purpose" describes program.

Key points:
- Applicable Feeds: EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data (in doc 5). In doc 6: BYX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data.
- Program provides fee waivers and lower data costs for both (i) Small Retail Brokers that provide Applicable Feeds to other Small Retail Brokers via hosted solutions (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) and (ii) Small Retail Brokers that receive this data from Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor (External Hosted Subscriber).

- Existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program provides discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed as well as discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data for eligible participants. Participants must be External Distributor meeting criteria: (i) broker-dealer distributing Applicable Feed to Non-Professional Data Users with whom broker-dealer has brokerage relationship; (ii) At least 90% of Distributor's total subscriber population must consist of Non-Professional subscribers, inclusive of any subscribers not receiving Applicable Feed; (iii) Distributor distributes Applicable Feed to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users (proposing increase to 10,000 for Cboe One Summary Data Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed).

Wait need check exact numbers for BYX in doc 6: Distribution Fee of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed, and Data Consolidation Fee $350/month. And max Non-Professional Data Users 5,000 increasing to 10,000.

Now proposed Program:
- Based on proposed eligibility criteria for Small Retail Brokers to specifically support Small Retail Brokers operating platforms on behalf of other Small Retail Brokers.
- There are Small Retail Brokers who want to provide data via hosted solution as White Label Service ("Hosting Small Retail Broker Dealer") to other Small Retail Brokers, who then provide this data to their retail clients (an "External Hosted Subscriber").
- Under existing structure, both External Hosted Subscriber and Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor are assessed standard discounted Distribution Fee (and for Cboe One Summary, discounted Data Consolidation Fee) under existing Small Retail Broker Program. These fees are in addition to standard Professional and Non-Professional User fees.
- Therefore existing fee structure does not allow additional benefits for Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors for providing valuable service of operating platforms that External Hosted Subscribers may use for their clients, and doesn't account for fact that Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors are also billed for fees of External Hosted Subscribers (which Small Retail Brokers under original program do not have).
- The Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and their users, and ultimately responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Subscribed Subscriber and itself.
- Proposed pricing allows Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors freedom to charge or not charge External Hosted Subscribers while also appropriately charging for service provided.

Specifically, under proposed program:
- A Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under existing Small Retail Broker Program.
- Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed).
- The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for Cboe One Summary Data (credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data. The Professional User fees shall remain the same.
- Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for program and will be required to directly license with Exchange for Applicable Feed.
- The 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on firm level (counts for each Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers looked at separately).
- The Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one exceeds threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program).

Full answer
-----------

To determine the hosting broker's monthly bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top feeds, analyze the fee structures from the proposed Hosted Solutions Programs in Documents 5 (Cboe EDGX) and 6 (Cboe BYX), considering eligibility caps and program rules.

Key Fee Parameters:

- EDGX Top Data Feed (Document 5):
- Standard Non-Professional User fee: $0.10/user/month (from "10,000 Users × 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000")
- Hosted Solutions Program:
- Hosting Broker (as Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor): Distribution Fee ($750/month) credited/waived; pays only user fees for its own users.
- Each eligible External Hosted Subscriber: Fixed fee of $750/month (replaces Distribution Fee + user fees).
- Eligibility cap: 10,000 Non-Professional users per firm (Hosting Broker and each External Hosted Subscriber assessed separately).
- BYX Top Data Feed (Document 6):
- Standard Non-Professional User fee: $0.025/user/month (from "10,000 Users × 0.025/Non-Professional User = $250")
- Hosted Solutions Program:
- Hosting Broker: Distribution Fee ($250/month) credited/waived if ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber; pays user fees for own users.
- Each eligible External Hosted Subscriber: Fixed fee of $100/month (replaces Distribution Fee + user fees).
- Eligibility cap: 10,000 Non-Professional users per firm.
- Existing Small Retail Broker Program (fallback if no eligible External Hosted Subscribers):
- Discounted Distribution Fee: $250/month.
- Plus user fees at $0.025/user/month.
- Current cap: 5,000 users (proposed increase to 10,000 not yet effective for this calculation).

Eligibility Assessment:

EDGX Top Feed:

- Hosting Broker own users: 5,000 ≤ 10,000 → eligible as Hosting Distributor.
- Subscriber 1 (takes only EDGX Top): 4,000 users ≤ 10,000 → eligible External Hosted Subscriber.
- Subscriber 2: 4,000 EDGX Top users ≤ 10,000 → eligible External Hosted Subscriber.
- Hosting Broker has 2 eligible External Hosted Subscribers → qualifies for Hosted Solutions Program.

BYX Top Feed:

- Hosting Broker own users: 5,000 ≤ 10,000 → eligible as firm.
- Subscriber 1: Takes only EDGX Top → 0 BYX Top users (not an External Hosted Subscriber for BYX Top).
- Subscriber 2: 10,500 BYX Top users > 10,000 cap → ineligible for Hosted Solutions Program.
- No eligible External Hosted Subscribers for BYX Top → Hosting Broker does not qualify for Hosted Solutions Program; falls back to existing Small Retail Broker Program.
- Hosting Broker own users: 5,000 ≤ 5,000 (current cap) → eligible for existing program.

Monthly Bill Calculation:

EDGX Top Feed (Hosted Solutions Program):

- Hosting Broker own user fees: 5,000 users × $0.10 = $500
- Fixed fee for Subscriber 1: $750
- Fixed fee for Subscriber 2: $750
- EDGX Top Total = $500 + $750 + $750 = $2,000

BYX Top Feed (Existing Small Retail Broker Program):

- Distribution Fee: $250
- Hosting Broker own user fees: 5,000 users × $0.025 = $125
- BYX Top Total = $250 + $125 = $375

Combined Monthly Bill = $2,000 (EDGX Top) + $375 (BYX Top) = $2,375

\boxed{2375}

Reasoning
---------

Let me analyze this problem step by step. I need to calculate the monthly bill for a hosting broker (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) who has:
- 5,000 users of its own on EDGX Top
- 5,000 users of its own on BYX Top
- Two hosted subscribers (External Hosted Subscribers):
  - Subscriber 1: 4,000 users on EDGX Top only
  - Subscriber 2: 4,000 users on EDGX Top and 10,500 users on BYX Top
- All users are non-professional

I need to find the host's monthly bill for the two feeds (EDGX Top and BYX Top).

First, let me understand the pricing structure from the documents. Looking at Documents 5 and 6, which describe the Cboe EDGX and Cboe BYX exchanges' proposed rule changes for their Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Programs.

From Document 5 (Cboe EDGX):
- For the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor: credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed (waiver of Distribution Fee)
- For External Hosted Subscriber: waiver of Distribution Fee ($750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed) and fixed monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top Data Feed (instead of per-user fees)
- Non-Professional User maximum: 10,000 for EDGX Top Data Feed
- The program is capped at 10,000 users for EDGX Top Data Feed

From Document 6 (Cboe BYX):
- For the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor: credit of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed (waiver of Distribution Fee)
- For External Hosted Subscriber: waiver of Distribution Fee ($250/month for BYX Top Data Feed) and fixed monthly fee of $100 for BYX Top Data Feed (instead of per-user fees)
- Non-Professional User maximum: 10,000 for BYX Top Data Feed
- The program is capped at 10,000 users for BYX Top Data Feed

Also, both documents mention that the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and their users, and ultimately responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscribers and itself.

Let me check if there are per-user fees mentioned for Non-Professional Users outside of the program.

From Document 5: "As the Program is capped at 10,000 users for Cboe One Summary Feed and 10,000 for EDGX Top Data Feed, this equates to a maximum, savings of $250 (10,000 Users x 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000 and $1,000-$750 = $250) for EDGX Top Data Feed..."

This suggests the standard Non-Professional User fee for EDGX Top is $0.10 per user per month.

From Document 6: "As the Program is capped at 10,000 users for Cboe One Summary Feed and 10,000 for BYX Top Data Feed, this equates to a maximum, savings of $150 (10,000 Users x 0.025/Non-Professional User = $250 and $250-100 = $150) for BYX Top Data Feed..."

This suggests the standard Non-Professional User fee for BYX Top is $0.025 per user per month.

Host's own users: 5,000

But the program cap is 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for EDGX Top Data Feed. Also: "The Exchange notes that the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on a firm level (i.e., the counts of the Non-Professional Data Users for each of the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers will be looked at separately). Additionally, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program)."

So the 10,000 cap applies per firm (per External Hosted Subscriber and for the Hosting Broker itself), not in aggregate.

Let's check each entity:

1. Hosting Broker (itself): 5,000 users on EDGX Top - under 10,000 cap, so eligible for program
2. Subscriber 1: 4,000 users on EDGX Top - under 10,000 cap, eligible
3. Subscriber 2: 4,000 users on EDGX Top - under 10,000 cap, eligible

All are under the 10,000 cap individually, so all can participate in the program.

For the Hosting Broker as a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor:
- Gets credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed (waiver of Distribution Fee)
- But what about per-user fees for its own 5,000 users?

- Each gets waiver of Distribution Fee ($750/month) and pays fixed monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top Data Feed (instead of per-user fees)

Wait, let me re-read: "Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). Participants of the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program must be an External Distributor that meets the following criteria: (i) Distributor is a broker-dealer distributing the Applicable Feed to Non-Professional Data Users with whom the broker-dealer has a brokerage relationship; (ii) At least 90% of the Distributor's total subscriber population must consist of Non-Professional subscribers, inclusive of any subscribers not receiving the Applicable Feed; and (iii) Distributor distributes the Applicable Feed to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users..."

So as a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor, it gets a credit (waiver) of the Distribution Fee that it would normally pay as a Small Retail Broker for its own distribution.

But it still has its own users, so does it pay user fees for its own users?

The document says: "the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and their users, and ultimately the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscribers and itself."

And for External Hosted Subscribers: "in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data."

But for the Hosting Broker's own users, it should pay the standard user fees, unless the Distribution Fee credit covers that.

Let's think about the NYSE analogy. Document 5: "a redistributor that meets the above requirements will both (i) pay a Per User Access Fee and (ii) have its redistribution fee waived."

And "Without these discounts, a redistributor of NYSE Trades would pay the General Access Fee of $1,500/month in addition to the Redistribution Fee of $1,000/month and the applicable Professional User Fee ($4/month/User) and Non-Professional User Fee ($0.20/month/User). Under these discounts, that same redistributor now only pays the Per User Access Fee of $100/month."

So for NYSE, the redistributor pays a fixed Per User Access Fee ($100/month) instead of General Access Fee + Redistribution Fee + per-user fees.

Similarly, for Cboe's new program, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor gets Distribution Fee waived (which is like Redistribution Fee), and External Hosted Subscribers pay fixed fee instead of per-user fees.

But what about the Hosting Broker's own users?

The key is: "the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscribers and itself."

And "the current Small Retail Broker Program prevents the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor from packaging this waiver as part of their overall service to their External Hosted Subscribers (as External Hosted Subscribers would be billed directly under the existing Small Retail Broker Program)."

Under the new program, the Hosting Broker pays for everything.

For the Hosting Broker's own users: since it's acting as a Small Retail Broker for them, it would normally pay Distribution Fee + user fees. But as a Hosting Distributor with at least one External Hosted Subscriber, it gets Distribution Fee credited (waived).

So for its own users, it pays only user fees (since Distribution Fee is waived).

For each External Hosted Subscriber: instead of them paying Distribution Fee + user fees, the Hosting Broker pays a fixed fee for them ($750 for EDGX Top, $100 for BYX Top per External Hosted Subscriber).

Let me confirm with Document 5: "Specifically, under the proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing the data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under the existing Small Retail Broker Program. Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for the Cboe One Summary Data (a credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data."

So:
- Hosting Broker (as Hosting Distributor): gets credit of its own Distribution Fee ($750 for EDGX Top)
- Each External Hosted Subscriber: gets Distribution Fee waived, and pays fixed $750/month for EDGX Top instead of user fees

But the Hosting Broker is responsible for paying all fees, so:
- For Hosting Broker's own users: since its Distribution Fee is credited, it pays only user fees for its own users
- For each External Hosted Subscriber: Hosting Broker pays the fixed $750/month (which covers what would be Distribution Fee + user fees for that subscriber)

The document says: "in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top" for the External Hosted Subscriber. And the Hosting Broker pays this on their behalf.

Also, "the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and their users, and ultimately the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscribers and itself."

So monthly bill for Hosting Broker = 
[User fees for Hosting Broker's own users] + 
[Fixed fee for each External Hosted Subscriber]

User fees are at standard Non-Professional rates: $0.10/user for EDGX Top, $0.025/user for BYX Top.

Fixed fee per External Hosted Subscriber: $750/month for EDGX Top, $100/month for BYX Top (from Document 6: "$100 for BYX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data")

Now, caps: each entity (Hosting Broker itself, and each External Hosted Subscriber) must have <= 10,000 Non-Professional users per feed to be eligible.

Check eligibility:

EDGX Top:
- Hosting Broker own users: 5,000 <= 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber 1: 4,000 <= 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber 2: 4,000 <= 10,000 ✓
All eligible for EDGX Top.

BYX Top:
- Hosting Broker own users: 5,000 <= 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber 1: 0 users (takes only EDGX Top) - but does it count? Subscriber 1 has 0 BYX Top users, so trivially <= 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber 2: 10,500 users > 10,000 ✗

Subscriber 2 has 10,500 BYX Top users, which exceeds the 10,000 cap.

Document 5: "Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed."

Document 6 same for BYX Top.

Also: "The Exchange notes that the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on a firm level (i.e., the counts of the Non-Professional Data Users for each of the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers will be looked at separately). Additionally, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program)."

So for BYX Top:
- Hosting Broker: 5,000 users <= 10,000, eligible
- Subscriber 1: 0 users <= 10,000, eligible (but has no BYX Top users, so maybe not participating in BYX Top program)
- Subscriber 2: 10,500 users > 10,000, NOT eligible for BYX Top program

Since Subscriber 2 exceeds cap for BYX Top, it cannot be an External Hosted Subscriber for BYX Top. It would need to directly license with the exchange for BYX Top.

Full answer
-----------

To determine the hosting broker's monthly bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top feeds, I need to analyze the fee structure under the Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program as described in Documents 5 (Cboe EDGX) and 6 (Cboe BYX).

Key Fee Structures:

EDGX Top Data Feed (Document 5):

- Standard Distribution Fee: $750/month
- Non-Professional User fee: $0.10/user/month (from footnote 12: "10,000 Users × 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000")
- Hosted Solutions Program:
- Hosting broker gets Distribution Fee waiver ($750 credit) if it has ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber
- Hosting broker pays per-user fees for its own users
- Each External Hosted Subscriber pays fixed $750/month (replaces Distribution Fee + per-user fees)
- 10,000 Non-Professional User cap per entity (hosting broker and each subscriber evaluated separately)

BYX Top Data Feed (Document 6):

- Standard Distribution Fee: $250/month
- Non-Professional User fee: $0.025/user/month (from footnote 12: "10,000 Users × 0.025/Non-Professional User = $250")
- Hosted Solutions Program:
- Hosting broker gets Distribution Fee waiver ($250 credit) if it has ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber for BYX Top
- Hosting broker pays per-user fees for its own users
- Each External Hosted Subscriber pays fixed $100/month
- 10,000 Non-Professional User cap per entity

User Analysis:

EDGX Top:

- Hosting broker's own users: 5,000 (≤10,000 cap → eligible as host)
- Subscriber 1: 4,000 users (≤10,000 cap → eligible)
- Subscriber 2: 4,000 users (≤10,000 cap → eligible)
- Hosting broker has 2 eligible External Hosted Subscribers → qualifies for program

BYX Top:

- Hosting broker's own users: 5,000 (≤10,000 cap)
- Subscriber 1: 0 users (doesn't use BYX Top)
- Subscriber 2: 10,500 users (>10,000 cap → ineligible per Document 5: "Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum..., the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program")
- Hosting broker has zero eligible External Hosted Subscribers for BYX Top → does not qualify for Hosted Solutions Program on BYX Top
- Subscriber 2 must license BYX Top directly from exchange (hosting broker not responsible)

Monthly Bill Calculation:

EDGX Top (Hosted Solutions Program applies):

- Hosting broker's own 5,000 users: Distribution Fee waived ($0) + 5,000 × $0.10 = $500
- Subscriber 1 (4,000 users): Fixed fee = $750
- Subscriber 2 (4,000 users): Fixed fee = $750
- EDGX Top Subtotal: $2,000

BYX Top (Standard Small Retail Broker Program applies):

- Hosting broker's own 5,000 users: Distribution Fee $250 + 5,000 × $0.025 = $250 + $125 = $375
- Subscriber 1: No BYX Top usage = $0
- Subscriber 2: Licenses directly (not hosting broker's responsibility) = $0
- BYX Top Subtotal: $375

Total Monthly Bill: $2,000 + $375 = $2,375

To determine the hosting broker's monthly bill for the EDGX Top and BYX Top data feeds under the proposed Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program, I need to analyze the fee structures described in Documents 5 (for EDGX) and 6 (for BYX), then apply them to the given scenario.

Key Fee Structures from the Documents

For EDGX Top (Document 5):

- Small Retail Broker Distribution Fee: $750/month
- Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor (with ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber) gets this $750 Distribution Fee waived/credited
- External Hosted Subscribers (with ≤10,000 non-professional users) pay a fixed monthly fee of $750 (instead of per-user fees)
- Host is responsible for payment of all data fees for its External Hosted Subscribers
- Cap: 10,000 non-professional users per entity (host and each hosted subscriber counted separately)

For BYX Top (Document 6):

- Small Retail Broker Distribution Fee: $250/month
- Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor (with ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber) gets this $250 Distribution Fee waived/credited
- External Hosted Subscribers (with ≤10,000 non-professional users) pay a fixed monthly fee of $100 (instead of per-user fees)
- Host is responsible for payment of all data fees for its External Hosted Subscribers
- Cap: 10,000 non-professional users per entity

Scenario Analysis

Hosting Broker (Host) has:

- 5,000 own users on EDGX Top
- 5,000 own users on BYX Top

Hosted Subscriber 1:

- Takes only EDGX Top with 4,000 users

Hosted Subscriber 2:

- 4,000 EDGX Top users
- 10,500 BYX Top users

All users are non-professional.

EDGX Top Feed Calculation:

1. Host's own EDGX Top distribution (5,000 users):

- Host qualifies for Small Retail Broker Program (5,000 ≤ 10,000 cap)
- Host has two eligible External Hosted Subscribers for EDGX Top (both under 10,000 cap)
- Therefore, host qualifies as Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor
- Host's $750 Distribution Fee is waived/credited → $0

1. Hosted Subscriber 1 (4,000 EDGX Top users):

- 4,000 ≤ 10,000 cap → eligible for program
- Pays fixed fee of $750/month
- Host responsible for payment → $750

1. Hosted Subscriber 2 (4,000 EDGX Top users):

- 4,000 ≤ 10,000 cap → eligible for program
- Pays fixed fee of $750/month
- Host responsible for payment → $750

Total EDGX Top bill = $0 + $750 + $750 = $1,500

BYX Top Feed Calculation:

1. Host's own BYX Top distribution (5,000 users):

- Host qualifies for Small Retail Broker Program (5,000 ≤ 10,000 cap)
- Hosted Subscriber 1 takes ONLY EDGX Top → no BYX Top subscription

1. Hosted Subscriber 1: No BYX Top subscription → $0
2. Hosted Subscriber 2 (10,500 BYX Top users):

- Document 6: "will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed"
- Host not responsible for payment → $0

Total BYX Top bill = $250 + $0 + $0 = $250

- BYX Top: $250
- Total: $1,750

Full answer
-----------

To determine the host's monthly bill for the EDGX Top and BYX Top feeds under the proposed Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program, the fee structures from the Federal Register documents (Documents 5 and 6) must be applied to the given user counts. All users are non-professional.

Key Fee Structures from Documents:

- EDGX Top Feed (Document 5):
- Non-Professional User fee: $0.10 per user per month (from Footnote 12: 10,000 users × $0.10 = $1,000).
- Distribution Fee: $750/month (waived for Hosting Broker with ≥1 External Hosted Subscriber).
- External Hosted Subscriber flat fee: $750/month (replaces Distribution Fee and per-user fees, capped at 10,000 users per firm).
- BYX Top Feed (Document 6):
- Non-Professional User fee: $0.025 per user per month (from Footnote 12: 10,000 users × $0.025 = $250).
- Distribution Fee: $250/month (waived for Hosting Broker with ≥1 External Hosted Subscriber).
- External Hosted Subscriber flat fee: $100/month (replaces Distribution Fee and per-user fees, capped at 10,000 users per firm).
- Eligibility Cap: 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users per firm per feed. Counts are assessed separately for the Hosting Broker and each External Hosted Subscriber.
- Hosting Broker Status: Requires ≥1 eligible External Hosted Subscriber for a specific feed to qualify for Distribution Fee waiver on that feed.

Given Scenario:

- Host's own users:
- EDGX Top: 5,000 users
- BYX Top: 5,000 users
- External Hosted Subscribers:
- Subscriber 1: 4,000 users on EDGX Top only (0 on BYX Top)
- Subscriber 2: 4,000 users on EDGX Top + 10,500 users on BYX Top
- All users non-professional; all counts per firm ≤10,000 except Subscriber 2 on BYX Top (10,500 > 10,000).

Analysis per Feed:

EDGX Top Feed

- Host qualifies as Hosting Broker (has 2 eligible External Hosted Subscribers for EDGX Top).
- Distribution Fee waived ($0).
- Host's own users (5,000): Pay per-user fee = 5,000 × $0.10 = $500.
- Subscriber 1 (4,000 users ≤ 10,000): Eligible External Hosted Subscriber → pays flat fee = $750.
- Subscriber 2 (4,000 users ≤ 10,000): Eligible External Hosted Subscriber → pays flat fee = $750.
- Total EDGX Top Bill = $500 + $750 + $750 = $2,000.

BYX Top Feed

- Host has no eligible External Hosted Subscribers for BYX Top:
- Subscriber 1: 0 users (not applicable).
- Subscriber 2: 10,500 users > 10,000 cap → ineligible for program (per Document 6: "exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum... no longer eligible... required to directly license with the Exchange").
- Host participates as regular Small Retail Broker (updated program with 10,000-user cap; Document 6 states cap increased to 10,000).
- Host's own users (5,000 ≤ 10,000): Qualify for discounted program.
- Distribution Fee applies = $250.
- Per-user fees for own users = 5,000 × $0.025 = $125.
- No fees for Subscriber 1 (0 users) or Subscriber 2 (ineligible; Host not responsible under program).
- Total BYX Top Bill = $250 + $125 = $375.

Total Monthly Bill:

- EDGX Top: $2,000
- BYX Top: $375
- Grand Total = $2,000 + $375 = $2,375

Full answer

$2,387.50

Reasoning
---------

Let me carefully analyze this question and the provided documents to determine the host's monthly bill for the two feeds.

The question is about a hosting broker with:
- 5,000 users of its own on EDGX Top
- 5,000 users of its own on BYX Top
- Two hosted subscribers:
  - Subscriber 1: 4,000 users on EDGX Top only
  - Subscriber 2: 4,000 users on EDGX Top and 10,500 users on BYX Top
- All users are non-professional

I need to find information about the fee structure for these feeds, particularly for hosting brokers and their hosted subscribers.

Looking at the documents, I see Document 5 and Document 6 discuss Cboe EDGX Exchange and Cboe BYX Exchange respectively, and both mention a "Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program".

Let me extract the relevant fee information from both documents.

From Document 5 (Cboe EDGX Exchange):
- The existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program has a discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed
- The proposed Program would provide a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed for Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors
- External Hosted Subscribers would also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee ($750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed)
- External Hosted Subscribers would pay a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top (instead of per-user Non-Professional User fees)
- The cap is 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for EDGX Top Data Feed

From Document 6 (Cboe BYX Exchange):
- The existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program has a discounted Distribution Fee of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed
- The proposed Program would provide a credit of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed for Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors
- External Hosted Subscribers would also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee ($250/month for BYX Top Data Feed)
- External Hosted Subscribers would pay a set monthly fee of $100 for BYX Top (instead of per-user Non-Professional User fees)
- The cap is 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for BYX Top Data Feed

Let me check if there's more specific information about how the fees work for the hosting broker itself (not just the hosted subscribers).

From Document 5:
"Specifically, under the proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing the data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under the existing Small Retail Broker Program."

So the hosting broker normally pays $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed under the existing program, but gets a credit of $750/month (so effectively $0) when it has at least one hosted subscriber.

Similarly, from Document 6:
"Specifically, under the proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker providing the data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (a credit of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under the existing Small Retail Broker Program."

So the hosting broker normally pays $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed under the existing program, but gets a credit of $250/month (so effectively $0) when it has at least one hosted subscriber.

Now, what about the per-user fees? The documents mention that External Hosted Subscribers pay a fixed monthly fee instead of per-user fees. But what about the hosting broker's own users?

Let me check Document 5 again:
"Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (a credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for the Cboe One Summary Data (a credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data."

And Document 6:
"Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive a waiver of the Distribution Fee (a credit of $250/month for BYX Top Data Feed and a credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for the Cboe One Summary Data (a credit of $350/month) and in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $100 for BYX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data."

But what about the hosting broker's own users? The documents don't explicitly state that the hosting broker pays per-user fees for its own users. Let me look for more information.

From Document 5:
"The Professional User fees shall remain the same. Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed."

And:
"The Exchange notes that the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on a firm level (i.e., the counts of the Non-Professional Data Users for each of the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers will be looked at separately)."

This suggests that the hosting broker's own users are counted separately from the hosted subscribers' users, and the 10,000 cap applies per entity.

But what fees does the hosting broker pay for its own users? Let me check the existing Small Retail Broker Program.

From Document 5:
"Currently, the Exchange offers a Small Retail Broker Distribution Program for both Applicable Data Feeds. This program provides a discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed as well as a discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data for eligible participants."

It mentions a "discounted Distribution Fee" but doesn't explicitly mention per-user fees for the existing program. However, it does say "in lieu of paying the Non-Professional User fees, it shall be a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top" for External Hosted Subscribers, implying that normally there are per-user Non-Professional User fees.

Let me look for the per-user fee amount.

From Document 5, footnote 12:
"As the Program is capped at 10,000 users for Cboe One Summary Feed and 10,000 for EDGX Top Data Feed, this equates to a maximum, savings of $250 (10,000 Users x 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000 and $1,000-$750 = $250) for EDGX Top Data Feed and $1,650 (10,000 Users x 0.25/Non-Professional = $2,500 and $2,500-850 = $1,650) for Cboe One Summary Feed."

Ah, here it is! For EDGX Top Data Feed, the per-user Non-Professional User fee is $0.10 per user per month.

Similarly, from Document 6, footnote 12:
"As the Program is capped at 10,000 users for Cboe One Summary Feed and 10,000 for BYX Top Data Feed, this equates to a maximum, savings of $150 (10,000 Users x 0.025/Non-Professional User = $250 and $250-100 = $150) for BYX Top Data Feed and $1,650 (10,000 Users x 0.25/Non-Professional = $2,500 and $2,500-850 = $1,650) for Cboe One Summary Feed."

So for BYX Top Data Feed, the per-user Non-Professional User fee is $0.025 per user per month.

Now, let's clarify the fee structure:

For the hosting broker (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor):
- Normally pays a Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top and $250/month for BYX Top
- But gets a credit of the full Distribution Fee ($750 for EDGX, $250 for BYX) when it has at least one hosted subscriber
- So effectively pays $0 for Distribution Fees for both feeds
- For its own users, it likely pays the per-user Non-Professional User fees: $0.10/user/month for EDGX Top, $0.025/user/month for BYX Top

For each External Hosted Subscriber:
- Gets a waiver of the Distribution Fee ($750 for EDGX, $250 for BYX)
- Pays a fixed monthly fee instead of per-user fees: $750/month for EDGX Top, $100/month for BYX Top
- This is regardless of the number of users (up to the 10,000 cap)

The question states: "the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and their users, and ultimately the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscriber and itself."

This is crucial! The hosting broker pays for everything - its own fees AND the hosted subscribers' fees.

So the hosting broker's monthly bill would include:
1. Fees for its own users (per-user fees)
2. Fees for Hosted Subscriber 1 (fixed fee for EDGX Top)
3. Fees for Hosted Subscriber 2 (fixed fees for EDGX Top and BYX Top)

Let's calculate:

Hosting broker's own users:
- EDGX Top: 5,000 users × $0.10/user/month = $500/month
- BYX Top: 5,000 users × $0.025/user/month = $125/month

Hosted Subscriber 1 (EDGX Top only, 4,000 users):

Calculating the Host's Monthly Bill

Setting Up the Facts

| Party | EDGX Top Users | BYX Top Users | |---|---|---| | Host (own users) | 5,000 | 5,000 | | Subscriber A | 4,000 | — | | Subscriber B | 4,000 | 10,500 |

All users are non-professional.

Eligibility Check (10,000 Non-Professional cap per firm, per feed)

- Host: 5,000 per feed — under 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber A (EDGX Top): 4,000 — under 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber B (EDGX Top): 4,000 — under 10,000 ✓
- Subscriber B (BYX Top): 10,500 — exceeds 10,000 ✗ → must directly license with the Exchange; no longer an External Hosted Subscriber for BYX Top

Since Subscriber A doesn't take BYX Top and Subscriber B is disqualified for BYX Top, the host has no External Hosted Subscribers for BYX Top and is not eligible for the BYX Hosted Solutions Program. For EDGX Top, both subscribers qualify, so the host is eligible.

EDGX Top Fee Breakdown (Hosted Solutions Program)

| Component | Amount | |---|---| | Host Distribution Fee (waived as credit) | $0 | | Host's own user fees: 5,000 × $0.10 | $500 | | Subscriber A fixed monthly fee | $750 | | Subscriber B fixed monthly fee | $750 | | EDGX Top subtotal | $2,000 |

(Standard Non-Professional rate for EDGX Top = $0.10/user/mo; fixed fee for External Hosted Subscribers = $750)

BYX Top Fee Breakdown (existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program)

| Component | Amount | |---|---| | Host Distribution Fee (discounted) | $250 | | Host's own user fees: 5,000 × $0.025 | $125 | | Subscriber B (directly licenses; host pays nothing) | $0 | | BYX Top subtotal | $375 |

(Standard Non-Professional rate for BYX Top = $0.025/user/mo)

Total Monthly Bill

Analysis

Key threshold issue: This Cboe EDGX filing (Document 5) defines the "Applicable Feeds" as EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data only. BYX Top is not an Applicable Feed under either the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program or the new Hosted Solutions Program. BYX Top is a product of a different exchange (Cboe BYX) with its own fee schedule, which is not provided in the document. Therefore, no program discounts or flat fees apply to BYX Top here, and its cost cannot be determined from the input documents.

---

EDGX Top – Monthly Bill

Eligibility (10,000 non-professional users per firm):

| Party | EDGX Top users | Eligible? | |---|---|---| | Hosting broker | 5,000 | ✓ | | Subscriber A | 4,000 | ✓ | | Subscriber B | 4,000 | ✓ (10,500 is on BYX Top, not EDGX Top) |

| Item | Amount | |---|---| | Host's own Distribution Fee | $750 − $750 credit = $0 (host has ≥ 1 External Hosted Subscriber) | | Host's own Non-Professional User fees | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | | Subscriber A (flat fee; Distribution Fee waived) | $750 | | Subscriber B (flat fee; Distribution Fee waived) | $750 | | EDGX Top total | $2,000/month |

BYX Top – Monthly Bill

Cannot be determined from the provided documents. BYX Top is not among the Applicable Feeds covered by this filing; the program's waivers, flat fees, and Distribution Fee credits do not apply to it.

Bottom line

EDGX Top: $2,000/month. BYX Top is not covered by this rule filing (the program only applies to EDGX Top and Cboe One Summary), so the host's full bill for both feeds cannot be calculated from the input documents. The 10,500 BYX Top users for Subscriber B are irrelevant to EDGX Top eligibility (which is assessed per-feed per-firm) and do not trigger any cap issue for this program.

Host's Monthly Bill Calculation

Fee Structure (from the BYX filing)

| Fee Component | BYX Top | (Assumed for EDGX Top) | |---|---|---| | Non-Professional User fee | $0.025/user/month | Same | | Discounted Distribution Fee (Small Retail Broker Program) | $250/month | Same | | Hosted Solutions fixed fee (per eligible hosted sub) | $100/month | Same | | Distribution Fee credit (hosting broker with ≥1 eligible hosted sub) | Full $250 waiver | Same |

(The filing covers BYX Top and Cboe One Summary as "Applicable Feeds." EDGX Top is offered by an affiliate with "substantially similar" top-of-book data; I apply the same fee schedule.)

---

EDGX Top

| Party | Users | Fee | |---|---|---| | Hosting broker (own) | 5,000 × $0.025 | $125 | | Hosted Sub 1 (eligible, < 10,000) | fixed | $100 | | Hosted Sub 2 (eligible, < 10,000) | fixed | $100 | | Distribution Fee | credited (has eligible hosted subs) | $0 | | EDGX Top subtotal | | $325 |

BYX Top

| Party | Users | Fee | |---|---|---| | Hosting broker (own) | 5,000 × $0.025 | $125 | | Hosted Sub 1 | doesn't take BYX Top | — | | Hosted Sub 2 | 10,500 > 10,000 cap → ineligible; must directly license with the Exchange | — | | Distribution Fee | no eligible hosted sub for BYX Top → no credit; falls back to existing Small Retail Broker Program | $250 | | BYX Top subtotal | | $375 |

---

Total

$$\$325 + \$375 = \boxed{\$700 \text{ per month}}$$

The key twist: Hosted Sub 2's 10,500 BYX Top users exceed the 10,000 Non-Professional cap, removing it from the program for that feed. With no remaining eligible hosted subscriber on BYX Top, the host loses its Distribution Fee credit and pays the standard $250 discount fee — while Sub 2 must license BYX Top directly from the Exchange at its own cost.

Full answer
-----------

Host's Monthly Bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top

Key Fee Parameters (from the EDGX filing)

| Item | Rate | |---|---| | Non-Professional User fee (EDGX Top) | $0.10/user/month | | Distribution Fee (EDGX Top, Small Retail Broker discount) | $750/month | | Distribution Fee under Hosted Solutions Program | Waived (for both host and hosted subscribers) | | External Hosted Subscriber fixed fee (EDGX Top) | $750/month (in lieu of per-user fees) | | Per-firm Non-Professional user cap | 10,000 per feed |

EDGX Top — Hosted Solutions Program Applies

All three firms are eligible (each ≤ 10,000 users on EDGX Top):

| Party | Users | Fees | Distribution Fee | Subtotal | |---|---|---|---|---| | Host (own) | 5,000 | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | $0 (waived—has hosted subscribers) | $500 | | Subscriber 1 (EDGX Top only) | 4,000 | $750 fixed | $0 (waived) | $750 | | Subscriber 2 (EDGX Top) | 4,000 | $750 fixed | $0 (waived) | $750 |

> EDGX Top total = $2,000/month

(Subscriber 2's 10,500 BYX Top users do not affect EDGX Top eligibility—the 10,000 cap is per feed, per firm.)

BYX Top — Program Does Not Apply

The Hosted Solutions Program covers only EDGX Top and Cboe One Summary. Standard rates apply for BYX Top (non-professional rate $0.10/user, Distribution Fee $750/month per party):

| Party | Users | Non-Prof fees | Distribution Fee | Subtotal | |---|---|---|---|---| | Host (own) | 5,000 | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | $750 | $1,250 | | Subscriber 2 | 10,500 | 10,500 × $0.10 = $1,050 | $750 | $1,800 |

> BYX Top total = $3,050/month

(Subscriber 2 exceeds 10,000 users on BYX Top and thus would not qualify for any hosted-solutions discount on that feed; standard per-user pricing applies.)

Grand Total

$$\$2{,}000 + \$3{,}050 = \boxed{\$5{,}050 \text{ per month}}$$

Full answer
-----------

Host's Monthly Bill: $450/month

Using the fee structure from the proposed Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program (Document 5), here is the breakdown for each feed. The key program features are:

- Distribution Fee: Waived for the host (it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber)
- Non-Professional User fee: $0.025/user/month (standard)
- External Hosted Subscriber flat fee: $100/month (replaces per-user fees), but only if the subscriber has ≤ 10,000 Non-Professional users on that feed
- 10,000-user cap is evaluated per firm, per feed

---

BYX Top

| Component | Amount | |---|---| | Host's Distribution Fee | $0 (waived) | | Host's own 5,000 users × $0.025 | $125 | | Hosted Sub 1 (0 BYX Top users) | $0 | | Hosted Sub 2 (10,500 BYX Top users) | $0 on host's bill — exceeds the 10,000 cap → must directly license with the Exchange | | BYX Top subtotal | $125 |

EDGX Top

| Component | Amount | |---|---| | Host's Distribution Fee | $0 (waived — has External Hosted Subscribers) | | Host's own 5,000 users × $0.025 | $125 | | Hosted Sub 1 (4,000 users, under cap) | $100 (flat fee) | | Hosted Sub 2 (4,000 users, under cap) | $100 (flat fee) | | EDGX Top subtotal | $325 |

---

Total: $125 + $325 = $450/month

Key point: Hosted Subscriber 2 has 10,500 BYX Top users, exceeding the 10,000-user threshold. Per the program rules, that subscriber must directly license BYX Top with the Exchange (paying those costs on its own bill), while remaining in the program for EDGX Top (4,000 users, under the cap). The host retains its program eligibility because it still has at least one External Hosted Subscriber.

The host now reports 15,500 non‑professional users in total (its own 5,000 plus the hosted 10,500). That crosses the 10,000‑user enterprise‑fee threshold for each feed, so per‑user pricing is replaced by the flat enterprise rate.

- EDGX Top: $10,000/month
- BYX Top: $10,000/month

Total monthly bill: $20,000 ($10,000 per feed).

Full answer
-----------

Answer: The host loses the hosted-solution fee waiver and falls back to the regular Small Retail Broker Distribution Program, so it pays the standard discounted distribution fees only:

- EDGX Top: $750/month
- BYX Top: $250/month

Total distribution bill: $1,000/month (plus the usual per‑user Professional/Non‑Professional access fees for the host’s own 5,000 subscribers).

Why:

1. Separate 10,000‑user caps – Under the Program, the host and each hosted subscriber are counted separately.

- Host: 5,000 Non‑Pro users (≤10,000) → still under the cap.
- Hosted subscriber: 10,500 Non‑Pro users (>10,000) → exceeds the cap.

1. Subscriber is kicked out – Once a hosted subscriber exceeds 10,000 Non‑Pro users, it is no longer eligible for the Program and must license directly with the Exchange. Because this was the host’s only hosted subscriber, the host no longer has any External Hosted Subscriber remaining in the Program.
2. Host loses the Program credit – A Hosting Small Retail Broker gets its Distribution Fee waived ($0) only while it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber in the Program. With that subscriber now direct‑licensed, the host loses that waiver.
3. Host reverts to the regular Small Retail Broker Distribution Program – The host still distributes to its own 5,000 Non‑Pro users (within the updated 10,000‑user limit), so it qualifies for the standard discounted distribution fees:

- EDGX Top Distribution Fee: $750/month
- BYX Top Distribution Fee: $250/month

(The Data Consolidation Fee ($350) applies only to Cboe One Summary, not to EDGX/BYX Top, and the subscriber now pays its own direct‑license fees, which are not part of the host’s bill.)

Reasoning
---------

We need parse input docs to answer: "A hosting broker with 5,000 non-professional users has one hosted subscriber, which grows to 10,500 non-professional users. What is the host's monthly bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top data after that?"

Need understand rules for Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program and existing Small Retail Brokerage Distribution Program, updates, eligibility, fees.

We have Document 5 (EDGX) and Document 6 (BYX) proposing program.

Need determine host's bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top data after hosted subscriber exceeds 10,000? Need understand fee structure and eligibility conditions.

Let's analyze documents carefully.

Document 5 (EDGX Notice of Filing and Immediate Effectiveness ... SR-CboeEDGX-2025-081):

Proposes Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program (Program) for EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data. Also updates existing eligibility requirements for Small Retail Brokerage Distribution Program for Cboe One Summary Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed.

We need extract fee schedules for EDGX Top and BYX Top (Document 6 similar).

Let's parse Document 5 details.

Program provides fee waivers and lower data costs for both (i) Small Retail Brokers that provide Applicable Feeds to other Small Retail Brokers via hosted solutions (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) and (ii) Small Retail Brokers that receive this data from Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor as set forth.

Also proposes to increase allowed max Non-Professional Data User subscriber count for existing Small Retail Broker Program for Cboe One Summary Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed. By way background, currently offers Small Retail Broker Distribution Program for both Applicable Data Feeds. Program provides discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed as well as discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data for eligible participants.

Participants of existing program must be External Distributor that meets criteria: (i) broker-dealer distributing Applicable Feed to Non-Professional Data Users with whom broker-dealer has brokerage relationship; (ii) At least 90% of Distributor's total subscriber population must consist of Non-Professional subscribers, inclusive of any subscribers not receiving Applicable Feed; and (iii) Distributor distributes Applicable Feed to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users (Exchange notes proposing to increase to 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data Feed and EDGX Top Data Feed).

Exchange now proposes new Program based on proposed eligibility criteria for Small Retail Brokers to specifically support Small Retail Brokers operating platforms on behalf of other Small Retail Brokers. Based on customer feedback, there are Small Retail Brokers who want to provide this data via hosted solution as White Label Service ("Hosting Small Retail Broker Dealer") to other Small Retail Brokers, who then provide this data to their retail clients (an "External Hosted Subscriber").

Under existing structure, both External Hosted Subscriber and Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor are assessed standard discounted Distribution Fee (and for Cboe One Summary, discounted Data Consolidation Fee) under existing Small Retail Broker Program. These fees are in addition to standard Professional and Non-Professional User fees. Therefore existing fee structure does not allow additional benefits for Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors for providing valuable service of operating platforms that External Hosted Subscribers may use for clients, and doesn't account for fact that Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors are also billed for fees of their External Hosted Subscribers (which Small Retail Brokers under original program do not have).

Of further note, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor responsible for reporting its External Hosted Subscribers and users, and ultimately responsible for payment of all data fees for both External Subscribed Subscriber and itself.

While Exchange not privy to pass-through costs between Hosting and External, proposed pricing allows Hosting freedom to charge or not charge External Hosted Subscribers while also appropriately charging for service provided to External Hosted Subscriber benefiting from infrastructure developed and supported by Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor. Exchange notes current Small Retail Broker Program prevents Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor from packaging this waiver as part of overall service to External Hosted Subscribers (as External Hosted Subscribers would be billed directly under existing Small Retail Broker Program).

Additionally, given External Hosted Subscribers are smaller relative to other Small Retail Brokers currently participating in existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program, ability to subscribe to Applicable Feeds as Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors likely not feasible. Specifically costs of Applicable Feeds and costs associated with building and maintaining technological infrastructure to receive and disseminate data may make access impractical. Generally speaking technology, infrastructure, connectivity costs are significant monetary investment and require significant human expertise and resources. As such totality of costs can make access to data difficult. Exchange believes proposed fees and ability to subscribe to Applicable Feeds as External Hosted Subscribers will make access more feasible. Indeed Exchange anticipates retail broker-dealers that seek to become External Hosted Subscribers are broker-dealers that do not have technological infrastructure in place to ingest and disseminate data as Hosting Small Retail Broker, likely smaller client bases and business models not conducive to investments necessary to become Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor.

In these regards, Exchange believes proposed program will incentivize Hosting Small Retail Brokers to offer Applicable Feeds to External Hosted Subscribers, making data accessible to larger number of broker-dealers and clients at affordable cost. Specifically, under proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for a credit of its Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under existing Small Retail Broker Program. Additionally, External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive waiver of Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). External Hosted Subscriber will also receive waiver of Data Consolidation Fee for Cboe One Summary Data (credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying Non-Professional User fees, it shall be set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data. Professional User fees remain same. Once External Hosted Subscriber exceeds Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for program and will be required to directly license with Exchange for Applicable Feed. Exchange notes 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on firm level (i.e., counts of Non-Professional Data Users for each of Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers will be looked at separately). Additionally, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one External Hosted Subscriber exceeds 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program).

In addition to changes above, Exchange also proposes to modify existing Small Retail Broker Program for Cboe One Summary Feed and EDGX Top Feed to increase number of Non-Professional Data User maximum from 5,000 to 10,000 to be consistent with proposed threshold for External Hosted Subscribers. As previously discussed, Exchange proposes to also use cap of 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for proposed Program. Exchange proposes to increase this in support of increased participation across both retail and investor markets in order to facilitate growth of smaller retail brokers on global scale.

The Exchange recognizes that Small Retail Brokers participating in existing Small Retail Broker Distributor Program are not eligible for proposed Distribution fee and Consolidation Fee waivers proposed to be offered to External Hosted Subscribers. Importantly, however, Exchange notes such incentives necessary to help encourage External Hosted Subscribers to connect to Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor, and in turn disseminate data to downstream retail clients. In doing so, Exchange believes Applicable Feeds will reach larger base of retail clients that may not otherwise have access to such data. As practical matter, by and between Small Retail Brokers in existing Small Retail Broker Distributor Program (i.e., those that take data directly from Exchange) and External Hosted Subscribers in proposed Program (i.e., those who take data from Hosted Small Retail Broker Distributor), former generally more sophisticated in terms of capital and technological infrastructure. As such, incentives such as fee waivers not necessarily required to encourage subscription to and dissemination of Applicable Feeds.

Comparatively, Exchange believes small retail brokers subscribing to and disseminating Applicable Feeds as External Hosted Subscribers would likely not, absent such incentives, otherwise even contemplate subscribing to and disseminating Applicable Feeds, thereby limiting availability of real-time trade and quote information that could otherwise be accessed by External Hosted Subscriber's end users.

Furthermore, as mentioned above, existing fee structure makes it costly for both Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributors and External Hosted Subscribers to provide data to External Hosted Subscribers' retail clients as Distribution Fees assessed on both Small Retail Brokers. Overall Exchange believes fee proposal will help make data more widely accessible for retail users who receive data from External Hosted Subscribers. Full answer
-----------

Short answer: The host loses the hosted‑solutions waiver and falls back to the standard Small‑Retail‑Broker distribution fees—$750/month for EDGX Top and $250/month for BYX Top (i.e., $1,000/month in distribution fees), plus the usual per‑user fees for its own 5,000 subscribers. The subscriber that hit 10,500 users is kicked out of the program and must license directly with the exchange.

How the rules work

| Item | EDGX Top (Doc 5) | BYX Top (Doc 6) | |------|------------------|-----------------| | Standard Small‑Retail‑Broker distribution fee | $750/mo | $250/mo | | Hosted‑solutions credit for the host | Waives that $750/mo fee | Waives that $250/mo fee | | Hosted‑subscriber flat fee (in lieu of Non‑Pro user fees) | $750/mo | $100/mo | | Program cap for Non‑Pro users | 10,000 (raised from 5,000) | 10,000 (raised from 5,000) | | Consequence of exceeding 10,000 | Subscriber is no longer eligible and must directly license with the Exchange. The counts are looked at per firm, so the host’s own 5,000 users are separate. | Same |

Key language from the filings

- Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds 10,000 Non‑Professional users, “the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange” (Doc 5, Doc 6).
- The host “shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber” — but that language applies when another subscriber remains under the cap. Here the host had only one hosted subscriber, and that one just exceeded the cap and left the program. So the host no longer has a qualifying hosted subscriber and loses the distribution‑fee credit (Doc 5, Doc 6).
- Because the host itself still has only 5,000 users, it still meets the updated Small‑Retail‑Broker Program eligibility (cap now 10,000 users) and therefore pays the discounted distribution fee, not the full retail/vendor rate (Doc 5, Doc 6).

Bottom line for the host’s bill

- EDGX Top: $750/month distribution fee (credit lost) + regular user fees for its own 5,000 subscribers.
- BYX Top: $250/month distribution fee (credit lost) + regular user fees for its own 5,000 subscribers.

So the host’s monthly distribution‑fee bill for the two feeds is $1,000, and its former subscriber (now 10,500 users) is billed separately as a direct licensee.
