Docs: Doc 5, Doc 6 · Note: —

Final answer: - Because this was the host’s only hosted subscriber, the host now has no eligible hosted subscribers and therefore loses the Hosted Program benefits (the distribution-fee credit). | Your answer (gold): $1,625

Passages quoted:

- Quoted Doc 5: “Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber”
- Quoted Doc 5: “The Professional User fees shall remain the same”

Named Doc 5, Doc 6

Full answer
-----------

Under the proposed Hosted Solutions Program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor keeps its distribution-fee waiver only so long as it has at least one eligible External Hosted Subscriber (counted separately at the firm level).

- The subscriber cap for the program (and for the regular Small Retail Broker Program after the proposed increase) is 10,000 Non-Professional Users.
- Once the hosted subscriber hits 10,500 users, it exceeds the cap, becomes ineligible for the program, and must license directly with the Exchange.
- Because this was the host’s only hosted subscriber, the host now has no eligible hosted subscribers and therefore loses the Hosted Program benefits (the distribution-fee credit).

The host itself still has 5,000 Non-Professional Users, so it remains eligible for the standard Small Retail Broker Distribution Program, but it must now pay the regular discounted distribution fees:

| Feed | Regular Small-Retail Distribution Fee | |------|---------------------------------------| | EDGX Top | $750/month | | BYX Top | $250/month |

So the host’s monthly bill for those two feeds is:

$750 (EDGX Top) + $250 (BYX Top) = $1,000 per month (plus standard Professional/Non-Professional user fees for its own 5,000 subscribers; the now-ineligible hosted subscriber bills the Exchange directly).

---------

Document 5 (EDGX) and Document 6 (BYX) propose to introduce Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program for Applicable Feeds (EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data in doc5; BYX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data in doc6).

Program provides fee waivers and lower data costs for both (i) Small Retail Brokers that provide Applicable Feeds to other Small Retail Brokers via hosted solutions (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) and (ii) the Small Retail Brokers that receive this data from Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor (External Hosted Subscriber).

Existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program provides discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed as well as discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data for eligible participants. Participants must be External Distributor meeting criteria: (i) broker-dealer distributing Applicable Feed to Non-Professional Data Users with whom broker-dealer has brokerage relationship; (ii) At least 90% of Distributor's total subscriber population must consist of Non-Professional subscribers, inclusive of any subscribers not receiving Applicable Feed; (iii) Distributor distributes Applicable Feed to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users (proposing increase to 10,000).

Now propose new Program based on eligibility criteria for Small Retail Brokers specifically to support those operating platforms on behalf of other Small Retail Brokers.

Under proposed program, a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber would be eligible for credit of its Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed) that it is normally responsible for under existing Small Retail Broker Program. Additionally, the External Hosted Subscriber shall also receive waiver of Distribution Fee (credit of $750/month for EDGX Top and credit of $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Feed). The External Hosted Subscriber will also receive waiver of Data Consolidation Fee for Cboe One Summary Data (credit of $350/month), and in lieu of paying Non-Professional User fees, it shall be set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary Data. Professional User fees remain same. Once External Hosted Subscriber exceeds Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for program and will be required to directly license with Exchange for Applicable Feed.

Exchange notes 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on firm level (i.e., counts of Non-Professional Data Users for each of Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers will be looked at separately). Additionally, Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one External Hosted Subscriber exceeds 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program).

Docs: Doc 5, Doc 6 · Note: —

Final answer: (The 10,500‑user subscriber is now direct‑licensed and is not on the host’s bill.) | Your answer (gold): $1,625

Passages quoted:

- Quoted Doc 5: “Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor”
- Quoted Doc 5: “at least one External Hosted Subscriber”
- Quoted Doc 5: “shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber”

Named Doc 5, Doc 6

Full answer
-----------

Under the hosted-solutions rules (Documents 5 and 6), each firm’s Non‑Professional user count is checked separately against the 10,000‑user program cap.

- The host has 5,000 Non‑Professional users → still within the cap.
- The hosted subscriber now has 10,500 → exceeds the cap, so it is no longer eligible for the Program and must license directly with the exchange.

Because the host has only that one hosted subscriber, once that subscriber is out of the Program the host no longer has an eligible hosted subscriber and loses the hosted‑program distribution‑fee waiver. It falls back to the standard Small Retail Broker Distribution Program for its own 5,000 users.

Fee structure for the host’s own users

- EDGX Top: discounted Distribution Fee $750/mo + Non‑Professional user fee $0.10/user
- BYX Top: discounted Distribution Fee $250/mo + Non‑Professional user fee $0.025/user

Host’s monthly bill

| Feed | Distribution Fee | User fees (5,000 users) | Monthly total | |------|-------------------|------------------------|---------------| | EDGX Top | $750 | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | $1,250 | | BYX Top | $250 | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 | $375 |

So after the subscriber grows to 10,500:

- EDGX Top: $1,250/month
- BYX Top: $375/month
- Combined: $1,625/month

(The 10,500‑user subscriber is now direct‑licensed and is not on the host’s bill.)

- New Program (Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program) for EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data.

Full answer
-----------

Under the proposed Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program (Documents 5 and 6):

- A Hosting Small Retail Broker gets a full waiver/credit of its Distribution Fee only while it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber in the program.
- An External Hosted Subscriber is capped at 10,000 Non-Professional Users. Once it exceeds that (here, 10,500), it is no longer eligible for the program and must license directly with the exchange.
- Because this host had only that one hosted subscriber, once that subscriber is out, the host no longer has any External Hosted Subscribers and therefore loses the hosted-program distribution-fee waiver.
- The host still has its own 5,000 Non-Professional Users, which is within the updated 10,000-user cap for the regular Small Retail Broker Distribution Program, so it falls back to that program’s pricing.

Fee structure after the change

| Feed | Program Distribution Fee | Non-Prof. User Fee | Cost for host’s 5,000 users | |------|--------------------------|-------------------|----------------------------| | EDGX Top (Doc. 5) | $750/mo | $0.10/user/mo | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | | BYX Top (Doc. 6) | $250/mo | $0.025/user/mo | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 |

Host’s monthly bill

- EDGX Top: $750 + $500 = $1,250/month
- BYX Top: $250 + $125 = $375/month

Total for both feeds: $1,625/month

So after the subscriber grows to 10,500 and is forced to license directly, the host reverts to the standard Small Retail Broker Distribution Program and pays $1,250 for EDGX Top and $375 for BYX Top each month (based on its own 5,000 non-professional users).

Full answer
-----------

Host's Monthly Bill After Subscriber Exceeds 10,000 Users

Key Rule Analysis

Both filings (Documents 5 and 6) establish that the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User cap is looked at on a firm level — each firm's count is assessed separately. When an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds 10,000:

- That subscriber is no longer eligible for the program and must directly license with the Exchange.
- The host remains eligible only "so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber" — the parenthetical confirms that with one of two subscribers exceeding, only the host and the other subscriber continue. With only one subscriber (who exceeds and leaves), the host has zero remaining and loses program eligibility.

Host Falls Back to Existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program

The host's own 5,000 non-professional users qualify (≤ 10,000 threshold). The external subscriber (10,500) directly licenses separately.

| | EDGX Top | BYX Top | |---|---|---| | Discounted Distribution Fee | $750 | $250 | | Non-Prof. User Fees (5,000 users) | 5,000 × $0.10 = $500 | 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 | | Monthly total | $1,250 | $375 |

Answer

> The host's combined monthly bill is $1,625 — $1,250 for EDGX Top and $375 for BYX Top.

The host loses the hosted-program Distribution Fee waivers and must pay the standard discounted Distribution Fees plus per-user fees for its own 5,000 non-professional users under the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program.

Reasoning
---------

Let me work through this step by step.

We need to figure out the monthly bill for a hosting broker (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) under the Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program, considering:

1. The host has 5,000 non-professional users
2. The host has one hosted subscriber (External Hosted Subscriber) that grows to 10,500 non-professional users

Let me look at the rules from Documents 5 (EDGX) and 6 (BYX).

**Key provisions:**

From both documents:
- The Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor is responsible for payment of all data fees for both its External Hosted Subscribers and itself.
- The 10,000 Non-Professional Data User count eligibility requirement is looked at on a firm level (i.e., counts for each of the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and each of its External Hosted Subscribers are looked at separately).
- Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum (10,000), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange.
- The Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber.

**EDGX Top Data Feed fees (Document 5):**
- Under existing Small Retail Broker Program: discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month
- Under the proposed program: Hosting Small Retail Broker gets a credit (waiver) of Distribution Fee ($750/month) when providing to at least one External Hosted Subscriber
- External Hosted Subscriber gets waiver of Distribution Fee ($750/month)
- External Hosted Subscriber gets set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top (in lieu of Non-Professional User fees) if eligible (≤10,000 users)
- Professional User fees remain the same
- Non-Professional User fee standard rate for EDGX Top: $0.10/user (from footnote 12: 10,000 Users x 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000)

**BYX Top Data Feed fees (Document 6):**

Full answer
-----------

Analysis

Based on Document 5 (the Cboe EDGX Exchange proposed rule change, SR-CboeEDGX-2025-081), here is what happens:

The hosted subscriber exceeds the program cap

The program caps eligibility at 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users per firm. With 10,500 users, the hosted subscriber exceeds the threshold and is no longer eligible for the program. Per the filing:

> "Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum…, the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed."

The host remains eligible only "so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber." Since its sole subscriber drops out, the host loses its Distribution Fee waiver and the program's benefits.

EDGX Top cost

The host falls back to the standard Small Retail Broker Distribution Program (it still qualifies with its own 5,000 non-professional users, under the 10,000 cap):

| Item | Cost | |---|---| | Discounted Distribution Fee (EDGX Top) | $750/month | | Non-Professional User fees (5,000 × $0.10) | $500/month | | Total for EDGX Top | $1,250/month |

(The $0.10/user rate is derived from footnote 12: "10,000 Users x 0.10/Non-Professional User = $1,000.")

BYX Top cost

The document does not provide any pricing for BYX Top data. The program described applies only to the "Applicable Feeds" — EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data. BYX is mentioned only as an affiliated exchange (Cboe BYX Exchange) whose data feeds into the Cboe One Summary product. BYX Top as a standalone product has its own separate fee schedule not included in this filing. Therefore, the host's total monthly bill cannot be fully determined from the provided documents, as the BYX Top component is outside the scope of this rule change.

Reasoning
---------

Let me analyze this question carefully.

The question asks about a hosting broker (Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor) with 5,000 non-professional users, which has one hosted subscriber (External Hosted Subscriber) that grows to 10,500 non-professional users. What is the host's monthly bill for EDGX Top and BYX Top data?

Let me look at Document 5 (the Cboe EDGX Exchange rule change) for the relevant details.

Key points from Document 5:

1. The Exchange proposes a Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program for EDGX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data (the "Applicable Feeds").

2. The program involves:
   - Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor: a Small Retail Broker that provides the Applicable Feeds to other Small Retail Brokers via hosted solutions
   - External Hosted Subscriber: the Small Retail Brokers that receive this data from a Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor

3. Existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program:
   - Discounted Distribution Fee of $750/month for EDGX Top Data Feed and $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary Data Feed
   - Discounted Data Consolidation Fee of $350/month for Cboe One Summary Data
   - Eligibility: must be an External Distributor meeting criteria including distributing to no more than 5,000 Non-Professional Data Users (being increased to 10,000)

4. Under the proposed program:
   - A Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor providing data to at least one External Hosted Subscriber gets a credit of its Distribution Fee ($750/month for EDGX Top, $3,500/month for Cboe One Summary)
   - The External Hosted Subscriber also receives a waiver of the Distribution Fee
   - The External Hosted Subscriber receives a waiver of the Data Consolidation Fee for Cboe One Summary
   - In lieu of per-user Non-Professional User fees, External Hosted Subscriber pays a set monthly fee of $750 for EDGX Top and $850 for Cboe One Summary
   - Professional User fees remain the same
   - If External Hosted Subscriber exceeds 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users, they are no longer eligible for the program and must license directly

5. The eligibility requirement of 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users is looked at on a firm level.

Now, the question says the hosted subscriber grows to 10,500 non-professional users. This exceeds the 10,000 cap.

Also: "Additionally, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber (i.e., if it has two External Hosted Subscribers and one External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the 10,000 Non-Professional Data User threshold, the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor and the other External Hosted Subscriber may still continue under this Program)."

Let me re-read: "the Hosting Small Retail Broker Distributor shall continue to remain eligible for this Program so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber." The External Hosted Subscriber exceeding 10,000 is "no longer eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange." 

So the External Hosted Subscriber drops out of the program. The hosting broker then has zero External Hosted Subscribers under the program (since the only one dropped out). So the hosting broker would no longer be eligible for the program (since the condition is having at least one External Hosted Subscriber).

Full answer
-----------

Analysis

Document 5 (Cboe BYX Exchange Hosted Solutions Program) provides the relevant rules.

Step 1: The hosted subscriber exceeds the cap

The program caps eligibility at 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users. The subscriber grew to 10,500, so it is no longer eligible and "will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed."

Step 2: The host loses program eligibility

The program requires the hosting broker to have at least one External Hosted Subscriber to remain eligible. The host had only one subscriber. Once that subscriber is forced out (and begins directly licensing with the Exchange), the host has zero eligible External Hosted Subscribers and drops out of the Hosted Solutions Program.

Step 3: The host falls back to existing pricing

The host has 5,000 non-professional users—within the 10,000 cap—and can still qualify for the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program:

BYX Top: | Fee | Amount | |---|---| | Discounted Distribution Fee | $250/month | | Non-Professional User fees (5,000 × $0.025) | $125/month | | Total | $375/month |

EDGX Top: Document 5 covers only BYX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data. EDGX Top is a separate product from a separate exchange (Cboe EDGX), and no pricing for it appears in the provided documents, so the host's EDGX Top bill cannot be determined from this filing.

Bottom line

The host's monthly bill is $375/month for BYX Top (Distribution Fee $250 + 5,000 × $0.025 non-professional user fees). The provided documents do not establish EDGX Top pricing; that would be governed by a separate exchange fee schedule. The subscriber, now at 10,500 users, must directly license with the Exchange and pay its own fees independently.

Analysis

Based on the Cboe EDGX rule change (Document 5), here's what happens after the hosted subscriber grows to 10,500 non-professional users:

Key provisions from the filing:

1. 10,000-user cap on External Hosted Subscribers: "Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 Non-Professional Data Users for Cboe One Summary Data and EDGX Top Data), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange."

With only one subscriber—and that subscriber now leaving to license directly—the host has zero External Hosted Subscribers and loses the Distribution Fee waiver.
3. Host falls back to the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program: With 5,000 of its own non-professional users (within the raised 10,000 cap), the host still qualifies for the standard discounted fees.

EDGX Top monthly bill for the host:

| Fee | Amount | |-----|--------| | Distribution Fee (discounted) | $750/month | | Non-Professional User Fee (5,000 × $0.10) | $500/month | | Total | $1,250/month |

Full answer
-----------

Analysis

This question tests understanding of the Cboe BYX Small Retail Broker Hosted Solutions Program rules (Document 5). Three key rules interact here:

1. The hosted subscriber's cap is exceeded. The External Hosted Subscriber grows to 10,500 non-professional users, exceeding the 10,000 maximum. Per the filing:

> "Once an External Hosted Subscriber exceeds the Non-Professional Data User maximum (no more than 10,000 …), the External Hosted Subscriber shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange for the Applicable Feed."

2. The host loses its only External Hosted Subscriber. The filing states the host remains eligible "so long as it has at least one External Hosted Subscriber." The host had only one, and that subscriber is now ineligible (it must direct-license). Therefore the host also loses program eligibility, including its Distribution Fee credit ($250/mo for BYX Top) and the fixed-fee structure.

3. EDGX Top is not an "Applicable Feed." The program covers only BYX Top Data and Cboe One Summary Data. EDGX Top is a feed of an affiliate exchange (Cboe EDGX) and is not addressed by this filing at all—its pricing and eligibility are unaffected by this program.

Result

- EDGX Top: Unchanged; not covered by this rule change. The host pays whatever standard EDGX Top fees apply (not specified in this document).
- BYX Top: The host (with its own 5,000 non-professional users) falls back to the existing Small Retail Broker Distribution Program (where it still qualifies with 5,000 ≤ 10,000):
- Discounted Distribution Fee: $250/month
- Non-Professional User fees: 5,000 × $0.025 = $125/month
- Subtotal for BYX Top: $375/month

The former hosted subscriber, now exceeding 10,000 users, must directly license with the Exchange at standard rates—but that bill is its own, not the host's.

Actually, I realize I should also consider whether the hosted subscriber being ineligible means the host can't count it as an "External Hosted Subscriber." The document says the subscriber "shall no longer be eligible for the program and will be required to directly license with the Exchange." So it becomes a direct licensee, not an External Hosted Subscriber under the program.

This means the host has zero External Hosted Subscribers under the program, and thus loses its Distribution Fee credit.

Let me also note: the hosted subscriber would now directly license and would be responsible for its own 10,500 users. But the question asks about the HOST's bill.

So the host's bill:
- BYX Top: Distribution Fee of $250/month (under Small Retail Broker Program) + 5,000 × $0.025 = $125 in user fees = $375/month
- EDGX Top: Not covered by this filing/program; standard EDGX fees apply (not specified in document)

But actually, I want to reconsider whether the host automatically qualifies for the Small Retail Broker Distribution Program. The criteria require the host to be an "External Distributor" distributing to "Non-Professional Data Users with whom the broker-dealer has a brokerage relationship." The host distributes to its own retail clients AND previously to the External Hosted Subscriber's clients. If the External Hosted Subscriber is now directly licensing, the host only distributes to its own 5,000 users, which should meet the criteria.

I think the answer focuses on the consequences of exceeding10,000:

1. The External Hosted Subscriber at 10,500 users exceeds the cap and must directly license with the Exchange.
2. The hosting broker (with only one External Hosted Subscriber) no longer has an eligible External Hosted Subscriber, so it loses the program's Distribution Fee waiver/credit.
3. The host reverts to the Small Retail Broker Distribution Program: $250/month distribution fee for BYX Top + user fees.
4. EDGX Top is NOT an Applicable Feed under this program - the program only covers BYX Top and Cboe One Summary. So EDGX Top pricing is unchanged and not specified in this document.
